☐يَجْرِي النِّيلُ مِنَ الْجَنُوبِ إِلَى الشَّمَالِ.
yajrii n-niilu mina l-januubi ila sh-shamaal.
The Nile flows north through Sudan and Egypt to the Mediterranean. Almost all Egyptians live within a few kilometres of it.
pb:يَجْرِي النِّيلُ مِنَ الْجَنُوبِ إِلَى الشَّمَالِ.
☐بَنَى الْمِصْرِيُّونَ الْأَهْرَامَاتِ قَبْلَ آلَافِ السِّنِينَ.
banaa l-miṣriyyuuna l-ahraamaati qabla aalaafi s-siniin.
The Great Pyramid of Giza was built about 4,500 years ago for the pharaoh Khufu. For nearly 4,000 years it was the tallest building on Earth.
pb:بَنَى الْمِصْرِيُّونَ الْأَهْرَامَاتِ قَبْلَ آلَافِ السِّنِينَ.
☐فِي السُّودَانِ أَهْرَامَاتٌ كَثِيرَةٌ أَيْضًا.
fi s-suudaani ahraamaatun kathiiratun ayḍan.
Further south the kings and queens of Kush ruled from Meroë, in Sudan, and were buried under more than 200 steep pyramids.
pb:فِي السُّودَانِ أَهْرَامَاتٌ كَثِيرَةٌ أَيْضًا.
☐الْأَزْهَرُ جَامِعَةٌ قَدِيمَةٌ جِدًّا.
al-azharu jaamiʿatun qadiimatun jiddan.
Al-Azhar in Cairo, founded in 970, is one of the oldest universities in the world that still teaches today.
pb:الْأَزْهَرُ جَامِعَةٌ قَدِيمَةٌ جِدًّا.
☐قَنَاةُ السُّوَيْسِ تَصِلُ بَيْنَ بَحْرَيْنِ.
qanaatu s-suwaysi taṣilu bayna baḥrayn.
The Suez Canal opened in 1869, joining the Mediterranean to the Red Sea, so ships no longer had to sail all the way round Africa.
pb:قَنَاةُ السُّوَيْسِ تَصِلُ بَيْنَ بَحْرَيْنِ.
☐بِلَادُ الشَّامِ عَلَى شَاطِئِ الْبَحْرِ الْمُتَوَسِّطِ.
bilaadu sh-shaami ʿalaa shaaṭiʾi l-baḥri l-mutawassiṭ.
The Levant (Bilad al-Sham) — Syria, Lebanon, Jordan and Palestine — lies along the eastern shore of the Mediterranean.
pb:بِلَادُ الشَّامِ عَلَى شَاطِئِ الْبَحْرِ الْمُتَوَسِّطِ.
☐نَشَرَ الْفِينِيقِيُّونَ الْحُرُوفَ.
nashara l-fiiniiqiyyuuna l-ḥuruuf.
About 3,000 years ago the Phoenicians of this coast spread an alphabet of 22 letters. The Arabic, Hebrew, Greek and Latin alphabets all grew from it.
pb:نَشَرَ الْفِينِيقِيُّونَ الْحُرُوفَ.
☐دِمَشْقُ مِنْ أَقْدَمِ مُدُنِ الْعَالَمِ.
dimashqu min aqdami muduni l-ʿaalam.
In 661 Damascus became the capital of the Umayyad caliphate, whose lands reached from Spain to Central Asia. Its Great Mosque was finished in 715.
pb:دِمَشْقُ مِنْ أَقْدَمِ مُدُنِ الْعَالَمِ.
☐الْبَتْرَاءُ مَدِينَةٌ مَنْحُوتَةٌ فِي الصَّخْرِ.
al-batraaʾu madiinatun manḥuutatun fi ṣ-ṣakhr.
Petra, in Jordan, was carved into rose-red cliffs by the Nabataeans more than 2,000 years ago.
pb:الْبَتْرَاءُ مَدِينَةٌ مَنْحُوتَةٌ فِي الصَّخْرِ.
☐فِي الشَّامِ زَيْتُونٌ كَثِيرٌ.
fi sh-shaami zaytuunun kathiir.
Olive groves cover the hills, and dishes from here — hummus, falafel, tabbouleh — are loved far beyond the region.
pb:فِي الشَّامِ زَيْتُونٌ كَثِيرٌ.
☐شِبْهُ الْجَزِيرَةِ الْعَرَبِيَّةِ صَحْرَاءُ وَاسِعَةٌ.
shibhu l-jaziirati l-ʿarabiyyati ṣaḥraaʾu waasiʿa.
Most of the Arabian Peninsula is desert. Saudi Arabia covers most of it, with Kuwait, Bahrain, Qatar and the Emirates along the Gulf coast.
pb:شِبْهُ الْجَزِيرَةِ الْعَرَبِيَّةِ صَحْرَاءُ وَاسِعَةٌ.
☐بَدَأَ الْإِسْلَامُ فِي مَكَّةَ.
badaʾa l-islaamu fii makka.
Islam began in Mecca, where the Prophet Muhammad received the first revelation around 610. In 622 he moved to Medina; this Hijra begins the Islamic calendar.
pb:بَدَأَ الْإِسْلَامُ فِي مَكَّةَ.
☐غَاصَ الرِّجَالُ بَحْثًا عَنِ اللُّؤْلُؤِ.
ghaaṣa r-rijaalu baḥthan ʿani l-luʾluʾ.
For centuries Gulf towns lived from pearl diving. Divers held their breath for a minute or more to gather oysters from the sea bed.
pb:غَاصَ الرِّجَالُ بَحْثًا عَنِ اللُّؤْلُؤِ.
☐اكْتُشِفَ النِّفْطُ فِي الْخَلِيجِ.
uktushifa n-nifṭu fi l-khaliij.
In 1938 oil was found in large amounts in Saudi Arabia and Kuwait. Within a generation, fishing villages became modern cities.
pb:اكْتُشِفَ النِّفْطُ فِي الْخَلِيجِ.
☐نُقَدِّمُ الْقَهْوَةَ وَالتَّمْرَ لِلضُّيُوفِ.
nuqaddimu l-qahwata wa-t-tamra li-ḍ-ḍuyuuf.
Guests are welcomed with Arabic coffee, poured from a dallah into small cups, and a plate of dates.
pb:نُقَدِّمُ الْقَهْوَةَ وَالتَّمْرَ لِلضُّيُوفِ.
☐الْمَغْرِبُ الْعَرَبِيُّ فِي شَمَالِ إِفْرِيقْيَا.
al-maghribu l-ʿarabiyyu fii shamaali ifriiqyaa.
The Maghreb, "the West", stretches from Libya through Tunisia and Algeria to Morocco and Mauritania on the Atlantic.
pb:الْمَغْرِبُ الْعَرَبِيُّ فِي شَمَالِ إِفْرِيقْيَا.
☐قَرْطَاجُ مَدِينَةٌ قَدِيمَةٌ فِي تُونِسَ.
qarṭaaju madiinatun qadiimatun fii tuunis.
Carthage, near today's Tunis, was a great trading city founded by the Phoenicians. It fought Rome for more than a century.
pb:قَرْطَاجُ مَدِينَةٌ قَدِيمَةٌ فِي تُونِسَ.
☐أَسَّسَتْ فَاطِمَةُ الْفِهْرِيَّةُ جَامِعَةَ الْقَرَوِيِّينَ.
assasat faaṭimatu l-fihriyyatu jaamiʿata l-qarawiyyiin.
In 859 Fatima al-Fihri founded al-Qarawiyyin in Fez, Morocco. It is often called the oldest university in the world that is still running.
pb:أَسَّسَتْ فَاطِمَةُ الْفِهْرِيَّةُ جَامِعَةَ الْقَرَوِيِّينَ.
☐الْأَمَازِيغُ مِنْ أَقْدَمِ سُكَّانِ الْمَغْرِبِ.
al-amaaziighu min aqdami sukkaani l-maghrib.
The Amazigh (Berber) peoples lived here long before the Arabs came. Their languages and culture are still part of daily life, especially in the mountains.
pb:الْأَمَازِيغُ مِنْ أَقْدَمِ سُكَّانِ الْمَغْرِبِ.
☐الْكُسْكُسُ طَعَامٌ مَشْهُورٌ.
al-kuskusu ṭaʿaamun mash-huur.
Couscous is shared from one big dish, and sweet mint tea is poured from high up to make it froth.
pb:الْكُسْكُسُ طَعَامٌ مَشْهُورٌ.
☐الْعِرَاقُ بَيْنَ نَهْرَيْنِ: دِجْلَةَ وَالْفُرَاتِ.
al-ʿiraaqu bayna nahrayn: dijlata wa-l-furaat.
Iraq is the land between two rivers, the Tigris and the Euphrates — the ancient Greeks called it Mesopotamia.
pb:الْعِرَاقُ بَيْنَ نَهْرَيْنِ: دِجْلَةَ وَالْفُرَاتِ.
☐بَدَأَتِ الْكِتَابَةُ فِي سُومَرَ.
badaʾati l-kitaabatu fii suumar.
In Sumer, more than 5,000 years ago, people pressed wedge-shaped marks into clay tablets: cuneiform, one of the first kinds of writing.
pb:بَدَأَتِ الْكِتَابَةُ فِي سُومَرَ.
☐كَتَبَ حَمُّورَابِي قَوَانِينَهُ عَلَى حَجَرٍ.
kataba ḥammuuraabii qawaaniinahu ʿalaa ḥajar.
About 3,750 years ago King Hammurabi of Babylon had his laws carved on a tall black stone for everyone to see.
pb:كَتَبَ حَمُّورَابِي قَوَانِينَهُ عَلَى حَجَرٍ.
☐كَانَتْ بَغْدَادُ مَدِينَةَ الْعِلْمِ.
kaanat baghdaadu madiinata l-ʿilm.
In 762 the caliph al-Mansur founded Baghdad. Its House of Wisdom gathered scholars who translated the science of the world and added to it.
pb:كَانَتْ بَغْدَادُ مَدِينَةَ الْعِلْمِ.
☐فِي الْعِرَاقِ نَخْلٌ كَثِيرٌ.
fi l-ʿiraaqi nakhlun kathiir.
Southern Iraq once had more date palms than anywhere on Earth, and in the marshes people still build houses from reeds.
pb:فِي الْعِرَاقِ نَخْلٌ كَثِيرٌ.
☐الْيَمَنُ وَعُمَانُ فِي جَنُوبِ الْجَزِيرَةِ الْعَرَبِيَّةِ.
al-yamanu wa-ʿumaanu fii januubi l-jaziirati l-ʿarabiyya.
Yemen and Oman share the south of the Arabian Peninsula, between the great desert and the Arabian Sea.
pb:الْيَمَنُ وَعُمَانُ فِي جَنُوبِ الْجَزِيرَةِ الْعَرَبِيَّةِ.
☐كَانَتِ الْقَوَافِلُ تَحْمِلُ اللُّبَانَ.
kaanati l-qawaafilu taḥmilu l-lubaan.
Ancient kingdoms such as Saba (Sheba) grew rich from frankincense and myrrh, carried north by camel caravans.
pb:كَانَتِ الْقَوَافِلُ تَحْمِلُ اللُّبَانَ.
☐سَدُّ مَأْرِبَ قَدِيمٌ جِدًّا.
saddu maʾriba qadiimun jiddan.
The great dam at Marib watered the fields of Saba for more than a thousand years.
pb:سَدُّ مَأْرِبَ قَدِيمٌ جِدًّا.
☐جَاءَتِ الْقَهْوَةُ مِنَ الْيَمَنِ.
jaaʾati l-qahwatu mina l-yaman.
Coffee drinking spread from Yemen in the 1400s. The Red Sea port of Mocha gave its name to a kind of coffee.
pb:جَاءَتِ الْقَهْوَةُ مِنَ الْيَمَنِ.
☐سَافَرَ بَحَّارَةُ عُمَانَ بَعِيدًا.
saafara baḥḥaaratu ʿumaana baʿiidan.
Omani sailors traded across the Indian Ocean as far as East Africa; for a time the Sultan of Oman ruled from Zanzibar.
pb:سَافَرَ بَحَّارَةُ عُمَانَ بَعِيدًا.

Playbook · history timeline (16+) 7 items

✓ArabicTransliteration · EnglishCorrection / note
☐الْهِجْرَةُ إِلَى الْمَدِينَةِ.
al-hijratu ila l-madiina.
In 622 the Prophet Muhammad and his followers moved from Mecca to Medina. This Hijra is year 1 of the Islamic calendar.
pb:الْهِجْرَةُ إِلَى الْمَدِينَةِ.
☐الْعَرَبُ فِي الْأَنْدَلُسِ.
al-ʿarabu fi l-andalus.
By 711 Arab rule stretched from Damascus to Spain, where al-Andalus would flourish for nearly 800 years.
pb:الْعَرَبُ فِي الْأَنْدَلُسِ.
☐بَيْتُ الْحِكْمَةِ فِي بَغْدَادَ.
baytu l-ḥikmati fii baghdaad.
In 762 Baghdad was founded. Its House of Wisdom made it the centre of learning of the world.
pb:بَيْتُ الْحِكْمَةِ فِي بَغْدَادَ.
☐سُقُوطُ بَغْدَادَ.
suquuṭu baghdaad.
In 1258 the Mongols sacked Baghdad. It is said the Tigris ran dark with the ink of its books.
pb:سُقُوطُ بَغْدَادَ.
☐الْعُثْمَانِيُّونَ فِي الْبِلَادِ الْعَرَبِيَّةِ.
al-ʿuthmaaniyyuuna fi l-bilaadi l-ʿarabiyya.
In 1516–17 the Ottomans took Syria and Egypt. For about 400 years most Arab lands were ruled from Istanbul.
pb:الْعُثْمَانِيُّونَ فِي الْبِلَادِ الْعَرَبِيَّةِ.
☐جَامِعَةُ الدُّوَلِ الْعَرَبِيَّةِ.
jaamiʿatu d-duwali l-ʿarabiyya.
After the First World War, Britain and France divided much of the region between them. In 1945 seven Arab states founded the Arab League in Cairo.
pb:جَامِعَةُ الدُّوَلِ الْعَرَبِيَّةِ.
☐الِاسْتِقْلَالُ.
al-istiqlaal.
Between 1946 and 1971 one Arab country after another became independent. Algeria won its freedom in 1962 after a long and bitter war.
pb:الِاسْتِقْلَالُ.
